Projection algorithm:
```
def project_box_sum(x, target):
    x=np.clip(x,0,1)
    for _ in range(60):
       s=x.sum()
       if abs(s-target)<1e-12: break
       # binary search tau for clip(x - tau)
       lo=-1; hi=1
       for ... maybe 40:
          tau=0.5(lo+hi)
          y=np.clip(x-tau,0,1)
          if y.sum()<target: hi=tau else lo=tau
    return np.clip(x-0.5(lo+hi),0,1)
```
This projects onto box and sum. 60 outer maybe overkill; binary search 40 enough. For many steps, O(40n)=OK. But SciPy minimize with many iterations maybe heavy. We can instead optimize in unconstrained and project final? But local gradient benefits projection.

Alternatively, for local gradient, use exact sum by adjusting one variable: after gradient step, `x = h - step*grad`; then add mean offset `avg = target - x.sum()`; then bound project via iterative distribute. But projection via binary search best.

Let's detail continuous local.

`compute_weights_and_grad(h, beta)` top-level:
```
def compute_weights_and_grad(h, beta):
    n=h.size
    c=np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')
    M=float(c.max())
    z=beta*(c-M)
    # exp may underflow okay
    e=np.exp(z-M? already max 0)
    s=e.sum()
    if s==0: w = zeros center? else w=e/s
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    center=n-1
    c0_grad=1-2*h
    grad += w[center]*c0_grad
    for r in range(1,n):
       wp=w[center+r]; wn=w[center-r]
       if wp+wn <= 1e-15: continue
       grad[:r] += wn
       grad[r:] += wp
       # product array
       if r==0 no
       # Instead of allocate prod each r, do slices:
       # grad[r:] -= (wp+wn)*h[:-r]
       # grad[:r] -= (wp+wn)*h[r:]
       grad[r:] -= (wp+wn)*h[:-r]
       grad[:r] -= (wp+wn)*h[r:]
    return M, w, grad
```
Check product derivative: For r>0, auto_r = Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-r} h[i+r]h[i]. Derivative wrt h[j]:
 if j>=r: term includes h[j]h[j-r].
 if j<r: term includes h[j+r]h[j].
So product array `p` with p[:r]=h[r:], p[r:]=h[:-r]. We subtract (wp+wn)*p. Yes.
Linear: positive c = suffix - auto. Derivative linear wrt h[j] =1 if j>=r else 0. Negative c = prefix - auto: derivative =1 if j<r else0. So grad add wp to j>=r, wn to j<r. Above does.
`c0` = Σh(1-h): derivative 1-2h. Good.
Weights normalize sum 1. `M` unscaled max counts.

But `np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')` at lags: verify center+r corresponds to c with h[i+r](1-h[i])? Let's test n small. For h=[a,b], 1-h=[1-a,1-b]. full np.correlate: center index1? center=n-1=1. index2? let's manual: correlate full length3. According to numpy: c_k for k=-1,0,1? I need mapping. Let's compute with property c_full = np.convolve(h, reversed(1-h))? Hard. But max and gradient correspondence with r sign may be misaligned if index offset. def local_continuous(h, time_limit, rng, beta_start=8.0):
    target = h.size/2
    h=project_box_sum(h,target)
    best_m=evaluate_count(h)
    best_h=h.copy()
    coefs=[10,20,40,80]
    for beta in coefs:
      for it in range(2000):
        if time.time()-start>=time_limit: break
        M, grad = grad_eval(h,beta) # but avoids weights? we need no w outside
        if M < best_m - 1e-12: update
        if np.linalg.norm(grad) < 1e-8: break
        # line search
        g2=np.dot(grad,grad)
        step=1.0/ (np.sqrt(g2)+1e-8) # maybe scale up
        # Also try step*4 etc
        accepted=False
        for mult in [1,2,4,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.0156,0.0078,0.0039]:
            s=step*mult
            x=h - s*grad
            y=project_box_sum(x,target)
            my=evaluate_count(y)
            if my < M - 1e-10:
                h=y; M=my; accepted=True; break
        if not accepted:
           # gradient norm maybe zero or step too small; try random zero-sum perturbation
           if it>5 and rng: 
              noise_scale=0.05*rng...
              noise=rng.normal(size=n); noise-=noise.mean(); y=project(h+noise_scale*noise,target)
              my=...
              if my < M*1.05+1e-3: # allow? or accept to escape? better only if after maybe local later
                  h=y
        # update best
```

```
def project_box_sum(x, target):
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,1.0)
    if abs(x.sum()-target)<1e-12: return x
    lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
    # We need tau such that sum clip(x-tau)=target. As tau increases sum decreases.
    # If target less than current sum, tau positive? yes.
    for _ in range(50):
       tau=0.5*(lo+hi)
       y=np.clip(x-tau,0,1)
       if y.sum()<target: hi=tau
       else: lo=tau
    return np.clip(x-0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
```
Actually if target larger than sum, tau negative. Initial lo? tau in [min(x)-1, max(x)] maybe. Use -2,2. For x in [0,1], tau range [-1,1] sufficient? Adjust a variable with slack.
```

# exact: d=target-y.sum(); idx largest slack if d>0 else smallest nonzero; y[idx]+=d (clip)
```

```
def enforce_exact(y,target):
   d=target-y.sum()
   if d>0:
      order=np.argsort(1-y)[::-1] # largest slack
      for i in order:
        add=min(d,1-y[i]); y[i]+=add; d-=add; if abs(d)<1e-14 break
   else similar
```
This ensures exact.

Let's detail MILP function:
```
def milp_binary(n, time_limit, current_t):
   import cvxpy as cp
   x = cp.Variable(n, boolean=True)
   pairs=[]; zvar={}
   idx=0
   for i in range(n):
      for j in range(i+1,n):
         z = cp.Variable(nonneg=True) # continuous [0,1]
         constraints z <= x[i], z <= x[j], z >= x[i]+x[j]-1, z <=1
         store pair key (i,j) and z
   # Map distance/list
   t = cp.Variable(nonneg=True)
   cons=[cp.sum(x)==n/2]
   # For each r
   for r in range(1,n):
      # pos count: sum x[r:] - sum z_{i,r+i}
      # We can build expression via loop or increment
      pos=0; neg=0
      for i in range(n-r):
         z = zvars[(i,i+r)]
         pos += x[i+r] - z
         neg += x[i] - z
      cons.append(pos <= t)
      cons.append(neg <= t)
   # Maybe t lower bound current_t? no
   obj=cp.Minimize(t)
   prob=cp.Problem(obj,cons)
   try: prob.solve(solver=cp.SCS? no MILP? Use cp.CBC or cp.SCIP)
```
Need pair z storage efficient: Since we need z for each i,r, can precompute a 2D array? n up to 52, initialize `zvars = np.empty((n,n), object)`; fill symmetry. But object arrays okay. For constraints, avoid double. Use list of z in 2D.
Memory small. For n=52, 2704 vars. `object` array maybe okay. Building 3 constraints per pair = 8k constraints for n=56, maybe heavy. Limit to n=46,48.

def binary_hill(h, time_limit, rng, max_scans=1000):
   n=h.size; target=n/2
   h = h.copy(); ensure binary balanced
   cur = max_count(h)
   best = cur
   for scan in range(max_scans):
      if time > limit: break
      ones = np.flatnonzero(h); zeros = np.flatnonzero(1-h)
      best_s=cur; best_pair=None
      # Greedy? instead of scan all, maybe random sample if many.
      for i in ones:
         for j in zeros:
            if h[i]!=1 or h[j]!=0: continue # after moves same h? We revert so same.
            h[i]=0; h[j]=1
            s=max_count(h)
            if s < best_s:
               best_s=s; best_pair=(i,j); cur=s? keep? If s==0 break
            h[i]=1; h[j]=0
      if best_pair:
         h[...] = swap; cur=best_s; update best
      else:
         # no single swap improves; try 2-swap or random perturbation
         # evaluate a random number of moves that may worsen, then continue with small limit? Better break to restart.
         if rng is None: break
         # perturb: apply 3-10 random swaps, then maybe hill again with same function? Need avoid deep worsen.
         for _ in range(rng.integers(3,8)):
            i=random one, j=random zero swap
         cur=max_count(h)
         # If worsened too much (>cur worst+5), revert? Simpler keep.
```

def binary_sa(h, time_limit, rng):
   n=h.size; cur=score; best=cur; best_h=copy
   temp=0.3? Objective max maybe 15, delta 1. Want accept slight worsen. Initial temp maybe 1.0, cool to 0.01.
   no_improve=0
   while time:
      i=random one; j=random zero; swap
      new=score
      if new <= cur:
         cur=new; if new<best: update; no_improve=0
      else:
         if rng.random() < np.exp(-(new-cur)/max(temp,1e-6)):
             cur=new; no_improve+=1
         else:
             revert; no_improve+=1? 
      if no_improve > 2000: temp*=0.9; no_improve=0
      temp *= 0.99998
   return best_h,best_m
```

```
def binary_score_penalty(h, top=3):
   c=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
   M=c.max()
   s=np.sort(c)[-top:].sum()
   return M + 0.01*s
```

Pseudo for binary:
```
def binary_refine(h, time_limit, rng):
    n=h.size
    h = make_binary_balanced(h,n)
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    cur=float(binary_true_score(h))
    best=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    temp=1.0
    stall=0
    step=0
    while time.time()<deadline:
       # every 2000 steps, run one full improving scan
       if step % 2000 == 0:
          found, h, cur = one_improving_scan(h, cur, deadline)
          if cur < best: update
          if not found:
              stall +=1
              # perturb
              if stall>2:
                  # random restart from best or strong perturb
                  h = best_h.copy()
                  for _ in range(rng.integers(3,10)):
                       do random swap
                  cur=score(h); stall=0
              else:
                  do random swap accepting worse? maybe
          else:
              stall=0
              temp *=0.95
          step += 1
          continue
       # random move
       ones = np.flatnonzero(h); zeros = np.flatnonzero(1-h)
       i=int(rng.choice(ones)); j=int(rng.choice(zeros))
       h[i],h[j]=h[j],h[i]
       new=score(h)
       if new <= cur:
          cur=new; stall=0; if new<best: update
       else:
          if rng.random() < np.exp(-(new-cur)/max(temp,1e-6)):
              cur=new
          else:
              h[i],h[j]=h[j],h[i]; cur old
       if new<best: update
       temp *=0.99997
       step+=1
    # final full scan while time
    while time.time()<deadline:
       h,cur = full_scan_one(h,cur, deadline_stop=False)
    # project? binary exact
```
But `one_improving_scan` full 1024 evals may take small time; doing every 2000 steps fine.

def continuous_refine(h, time_limit, rng):
    target = h.size/2.0
    h = project_box_sum(h,target).copy()
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    best_h=h.copy(); best_M=score_count(h)
    beta_list=[6.0,15.0,40.0,100.0]
    for beta in beta_list:
       stalled=0
       for it in range(5000):
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          M, grad = grad_score(h,beta)
          if M < best_M: update
          gmax=np.max(np.abs(grad))
          if gmax < 1e-9: break
          step = 0.05 / gmax
          accepted=False
          for mult in (2.0,1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.0156,0.0078,0.0039):
              s=step*mult
              y=project_box_sum(h - s*grad, target)
              yM=score_count(y)
              if yM < M - 1e-10:
                  h=y; M=yM; accepted=True; break
          if not accepted:
              # small random perturbation
              if it>10:
                 scale = 0.02
                 noise=rng.normal(scale=scale,size=h.size)
                 noise -= noise.mean()
                 y=project_box_sum(h+noise, target)
                 yM=score_count(y)
                 if yM < M + 0.05 or yM < best_M:
                    h=y; M=yM
              if M > best_M + 0.1:
                 h=best_h.copy(); M=best_M
              stalled +=1
              if stalled > 100: break
          else:
              stalled=0
              if M == best_M? 
    # final deterministic coordinate? maybe.
    return best_h, best_M*dx
```

Let's implement coordinate descent for exact sum preserving pairs:
For continuous h (float), choose i,j. Let s = h[i]+h[j]. We can vary x = h[i], y = s - x, x∈[max(0,s-1), min(1,s)]. Objective as function of x: max of quadratics. We can sample grid (e.g. 31 points) and move to best. This is sum-preserving and bounds. If we sweep many pairs, can improve.
How to evaluate candidate fast? Could recompute full M O(n^2). For n=47, 2209 ops. Number pairs ~1100; grid21 =>23k evals per full sweep, each 2k ops=46M C ops, okay maybe 0.5s. We can do limited sweeps/pair samples. This can move values gradually while preserving sum, and can escape flat because second-order handled by grid. It may find small improvement. We can combine with gradient.

But implementing pair coordinate descent top-level. For each pair (i,j), choose grid including current and endpoints, maybe 9 points. Evaluate M for each candidate, choose min per pair. After row/col updates, objective nonincreasing. This is like block coordinate descent for nonconvex max; may converge. It can also handle flat c0 because sampling moves away.

However full pair scans O(n^2 * grid * correlate) expensive if repeated many. For n=47, maybe 1000 pairs*9*2209=20M ops per sweep; 10 sweeps 200M, okay. But with Python loops and correlate 9000 calls per sweep; maybe ~0.1-0.3s? Actually 9000 correlate 47 ~ maybe 0.2s. Could do 100s =500 sweeps. Good. This may reliably improve.

We can restrict to pairs (i,j) where values differ or selected. For n=47, use all i<j maybe. To avoid O(n^2) each sweep, sample random pairs 200 per iteration. Use random pairs and grid, update if improvement. This is stochastic coordinate descent. It can preserve sum exactly without projection, except float adjust. It may move to fractional. Algorithm `pair_cd(h, time_limit, rng)`:
- h float, sum exact.
- Loop while time:
   - not improved cycle? Use current M.
   - choose number of pairs per cycle: e.g. min(n*(n-1)//2, 300)
   - For each pair (i,j) maybe random distinct:
        s = h[i]+h[j]
        lo=max(0,s-1); hi=min(1,s)
        if hi-lo<1e-9: continue
        # grid: 9 points including current
        xs = np.linspace(lo,hi,9); insert current.
        best_x=h[i], best_m=M? We can evaluate current once? We have M.
        for x in xs:
           if x==h[i]: continue
           y=s-x
           h[i]=x; h[j]=y
           m=score_count(h)
           if m<best_m: best...
        set best
        if best_m < M: M=best_m; improved=True; update best
   - If no improvement after full random cycle, do a small random perturbation zero-sum, or switch to gradient.
- Ensure sum: adjusting pair preserves sum exactly except rounding; after each set, `h[i]=x; h[j]=s-x`; sum unchanged mathematically, but floating may drift by 1e-16. At end enforce exact.

This coordinate descent objective evaluation per candidate can be sped by only doing if potential lower? Could evaluate all grid. For n=47, grid 9, random pairs 500 =>4500 correlate per epoch. 100 epochs=450k evals, okay. `np.correlate` 47 ~2k ops, 900M ops maybe several seconds. Within 1100s.

def pair_cd(h, time_limit, rng):
    n = h.size
    target = n/2.0
    h = project_box_sum(h, target).copy()
    M = score_count(h)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_M=M
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    grid_points = 9
    pairs_per_cycle = min(n*(n-1)//2, max(50, 2*n))
    no_improve_cycles=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        improved=False
        for _ in range(pairs_per_cycle):
            if time.time()>deadline: break
            i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n)); if i==j continue
            s=h[i]+h[j]
            lo=max(0.0,s-1.0); hi=min(1.0,s)
            if hi-lo < 1e-8: continue
            # grid: use 11 points maybe, include current
            xs = np.linspace(lo, hi, 9)
            # add current value relative? not needed if current not on grid? Could evaluate current M
            best_x = h[i]; best_m = M
            for x in xs:
                x=float(x)
                y=s-x
                if not (0<=x<=1 and 0<=y<=1): continue
                if abs(x-h[i])<1e-12: continue
                h[i]=x; h[j]=y
                m=score_count(h)
                if m < best_m - 1e-12:
                    best_m=m; best_x=x; best_y=y
                h[i]=h0? Need store original? We overwrite; at end set best. Candidate evaluations leave h modified; for next candidate just set new x,y. After loop set h to best. To evaluate current, we don't need h. But during candidates, if we don't set before score, okay we set. Need keep original for final if no improve:
            orig_i=h_orig; orig_j=s-orig_i
            if best_x != orig_i: h[i]=best_x; h[j]=best_y; M=best_m; improved=True
            else: h[i]=orig_i; h[j]=orig_j
        if improved:
           no_improve_cycles=0
           if M < best_M: best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
        else:
           no_improve_cycles +=1
           # perturb zero-sum small if not too stagnant
           if no_improve_cycles > 3:
              scale = 0.05
              noise=rng.normal(scale=scale, size=n)
              noise -= noise.mean()
              y=project_box_sum(h + noise, target)
              yM=score_count(y)
              if yM < best_M + 0.2:
                 h=y; M=yM
              else:
                 h=best_h.copy(); M=best_M
              no_improve_cycles=0
           # occasionally add pair with more grid?
    # final exact sum, return best_h,best_M*(2/n)

def binary_refine(h, time_limit, rng):
    h = make_binary_balanced_int(h, h.size)
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    cur=binary_score_int(h)
    best_h=h.copy(); best=cur

       # SA phase for 2s? maybe use full scan for x seconds
       improved, cur = binary_full_scan_once(h,cur, deadline)
       if improved:
          stall=0
          if cur<best: update; continue
       # no single swap improves
       # Try random perturbation of k random swaps
       k=int(rng.integers(2, max(3, 1+cur//2)))  # e.g. 2-6
       for _ in range(k):
           i=random one; j=random zero; swap
       cur=binary_score_int(h)
       stall +=1
       if cur < best: update (unlikely)
       if cur > best + k + 5: # too bad, revert best
           h=best_h.copy(); cur=binary_score_int(h)
       if stall>8:
           # restart from best with more random
           h=best_h.copy(); cur=binary_score_int(h)
       # Also if time >? do a short SA? Full scan may repeat no improvement; perturbation.
    # final full scans until improve? while time:

This may oscillate. Maybe implement SA instead of just full scan repetitive. Let's add simple SA function:
```
def binary_sa(h, time_limit, rng):
   n = h.size
   start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
   cur=score; best=cur; best_h=h
   temp=1.0
   stall=0
   while time<deadline:
      # sample i from ones, j from zeros. Use precomputed? Every step flatnonzero costly. Instead arrays fixed set of ones/zeros? h changes. But can pick random positions and adjust: choose random x,y; if h[x]!=h[y], swap else resample? Since balanced, random two positions 50% different. Faster.
      x=int(rng.integers(n)); y=int(rng.integers(n))
      if h[x]==h[y]: continue
      h[x],h[y]=h[y],h[x]
      new=score
      if new <= cur:
         cur=new; stall=0
         if new<best: update
      else:
         if rng.random() < math.exp(-(new-cur)/max(temp,0.01)):
            cur=new; stall+=1
         else:
            h[x],h[y]=h[y],h[x]
            stall+=1
      if stall > 5000:
         temp *= 0.5; stall=0
      temp *= 0.99995
      if stall > 50000: break? 
   # final full scan improve
   while time<deadline and:
       improved, cur = binary_full_scan_once(h, cur, deadline)
       if improved and cur<best: update else break
   return best_h,best
```
SA random resampling of two positions has low efficiency (50% no move). But okay.
Then in `binary_refine`, do SA for a slice, then full scan repeatedly until no improvement few times, update best.

```
for start_h in starts:
   if time left < 60 break
   time_slice = min(60, remaining)
   best_h_try,best_try = binary_sa(start_h, time_slice*0.6, rng)
   # polish with full scans for remaining slice
   h=best_h_try; cur=score
   deadline2=time_slice
   while time<deadline2:
       improved,cur = binary_full_scan_once(h,cur,deadline2)
       if improved and cur<best: update; if cur<=target maybe break
       elif not improved:
           # small perturbation
           break
```
Need not overcomplicate. Maybe simpler: run SA for one big time (e.g. 60% remaining) from a single best starting point (structured best among quick evaluates), then full scan. But multiple starts helpful.

Let's refine functions for speed:

`score_count(h)`:
```
def score_count(h):
    v = 1.0 - h
    c = np.correlate(h, v, mode='full')
    return float(c.max())
```
For binary int, use separate:
```
def score_int(h):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode='full')
    return int(c.max())
```

`binary_full_scan_once_incremental(c,h,cur)`:
```
n=len(h); center=n-1
best=cur; best_i=None; best_newc=None
ones = np.flatnonzero(h)
zeros = np.flatnonzero(1-h)
for i in ones:
  for j in zeros:
     if h[i]!=1 or h[j]!=0: continue (h unchanged)
     newc = c.copy()
     # loop d
     for d in range(1,n):
        delta=0
        # pos +d
        if i+d < n: delta -= (1 - h[i+d])
        if i-d >=0: delta += h[i-d]
        if j+d < n: delta += (1 - h[j+d])
        if j-d >=0: delta -= h[j-d]
        if delta: newc[center+d] += delta
        # neg -d
        delta=0
        if i-d >=0: delta += (1 - h[i-d])  # -δ_i = +
        if i+d < n: delta += h[i+d]         #? for i dest from i+d: -δ_i*h_i+d = +h
        if j-d >=0: delta += (1 - h[j-d])  # δ_j=+
        if j+d < n: delta -= h[j+d]
        if delta: newc[center-d] += delta
     dist = abs(i-j)
     newc[center+dist] += 1
     newc[center-dist] += 1
     s = int(newc.max())
     if s < best:
        best=s; best_i=i; best_j=j; best_newc=newc
        if best <= target_count? maybe break? For n48 if best<=9 can't get below? Could still 8. Continue maybe.
     # revert no need
if best_i is not None:
   h[best_i]=0; h[best_j]=1; c = best_newc
   return True,c,best
return False,c,cur
```

Now, binary optimization can be very effective. Let's design `binary_optimize_from(h, time_limit, rng)` using incremental:
- Start h int balanced, c recompute, cur max.
- best.
- Loop phases:
   A. Full scan improving until no improvement (could be many scans). Each full scan can find one best single swap. Reapply until no improve. Because each swap reduces max integer by at least1. Starting max maybe15 to9, at most6 improvements? But after each swap, new improvement maybe small. Full scans fast, could converge quickly. However if stuck with no single swap, need perturb.
   B. Perturb: apply random K swaps (e.g. 2-5) accepting if not too bad? Then full scan again. Repeat if time.
This is a simple evolution. Since full scans fast, we can do many.

Pseudo:
```
def binary_optimize(h, time_limit, rng):
   n=h.size; h=make_binary...; c=init; cur=max
   start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
   best=cur; best_h=h.copy()
   # maybe target_min = floor(0.3808*n/2) but we just minimize
   rounds=0
   while time<deadline:
       # hill climb full scans
       hill_improved=False
       while time<deadline:
           # To avoid long if one full scan no improvement? full scan gives best improving move
           improved, c, cur = binary_full_scan_once(c,h)
           if improved:
               hill_improved=True
               if cur<best: update
               continue
           else:
               break
       if not hill_improved:
           # perturb best_h or current? Use current (local optimum)
           h = best_h.copy(); c=init; cur=score
           # random K swaps, choose K based on cur-best? maybe 2+rand
           k = int(rng.integers(2,6))
           for _ in range(k): apply random swap with c update (use candidate_max and accept directly because perturbation)
           cur = c.max()
           # If perturbation too bad (>cur+k), revert
           if cur > best + k + 2:
               h=best_h.copy(); c=init; cur=best
           # optional: if time left small, skip
       rounds+=1
   return best_h,best
```
But if after perturbation current local, we loop hill again. Multiple perturbations from best may get stuck in same. We can randomize h from best each time.

Could use SA instead of K-swap perturb to explore more. But K-swap + full scan is like iterated local search (ILS). Likely good.

Random swap apply with c update:
```
def apply_random_swap(h,c,rng,n):
   while True:
      x=rng.integers(n); y=rng.integers(n)
      if x!=y and h[x]!=h[y]: break
   if h[x]==1: i=x;j=y else i=y;j=x
   newc, s = binary_candidate_max(c,h,i,j,n)
   c=newc; h[i]=0; h[j]=1; return s
```
For K perturbation, apply K random swaps regardless.

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
  start = time.time(); deadline = start + max(5.0, float(budget_s) - 15.0)
  rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
  best_h=None; best_c=inf; best_n=0
  # initial
  init = globals().get('initial_h_values', None)
  if init is not None:
     h = np.asarray(init, dtype=float).copy()
     n = h.size; target=n/2.0; h=project_box_sum(h,target); exact_sum
     c=c5...
     update
     if remaining>60: pair_cd(h, min(150, remaining-60), rng) update
  # binary n=48 etc
  for n in [48, 50, 52, 56]? allocate slices.
     if remaining < 80: break
     time_slice = min(250, remaining)
     h0 = select_binary_start(n, rng)
     bh,bm = binary_optimize(h0, time_slice, rng)
     c=bm*(2/n); update

  # final continuous gradient on best maybe
  if best_h is not None and remaining >100:
     n=best_h.size; h=best_h.astype(float); hpair = pair_cd(h, min(150,remaining), rng); update
  # ensure exact sum and within [0,1]
  if best_h None: fallback random binary 48? generate and evaluate
  return best_h, best_c, best_n
```

`exact_sum` function:
```
def enforce_exact_sum(h, target):
    h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    # If target integer/ half, choose last adjustment
    for _ in range(3):
       s = h.sum()
       d = target - s
       if abs(d) < 1e-12: break
       if d > 0:
          idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
          add = min(1.0 - h[idx], d)
          h[idx] += add
       else:
          idx = int(np.argmax(h))
          add = min(h[idx], -d)
          h[idx] -= add
    # final set last
    if h.size > 0:
       val = target - h[:-1].sum()
       if 0.0 <= val <= 1.0:
          h[-1] = val
       else:
          # project?
          h = project_box_sum(h,target)
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
But setting last may violate [0,1] if val outside; but if sum close, should be inside if current nearly target and values within. If not, project.

`project_box_sum` with binary search exact? Let's implement efficiently:
```
def project_box_sum(x, target):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(x, dtype=float),0.0,1.0)
    target = float(target)
    if abs(x.sum()-target) < 1e-12:
        return enforce_exact_sum(x,target)
    lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
    for _ in range(40):
       tau = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       y = np.clip(x - tau, 0.0, 1.0)
       s = y.sum()
       if s < target:
          hi = tau
       else:
          lo = tau
    y = np.clip(x - 0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    return enforce_exact_sum(y,target)
```
Check tau direction: If target > sum, need decrease tau (x - tau larger). If s < target, tau too high? Example x sum 5,target6. tau=0 gives5<6 => hi=0? Starting lo=-1,hi=1. Need tau negative. If s<target, tau should lower (more negative). Our update hi=tau would move midpoint lower? Start tau0 mid=0, s<target => hi=0, next tau=-0.5, s maybe >target, lo=-0.5. Good. If s>target, tau higher, lo=tau. Works.

`enforce_exact_sum` should not call project_box_sum if val outside? It could recursion. Instead if val outside, just distribute. Keep simple.

For binary int, sum exact no need.

Potential issue: `np.correlate` for int arrays with `1 - h` if h dtype int64 returns int64; for large n counts up to 64? fine. For n maybe 64, int64.

Let's detail binary functions:

```
def initial_binary_c(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode='full').astype(np.int64)

def binary_candidate(c, h, i, j, n):
    center = n-1
    newc = c.copy()
    # Use local h continuous? h int values 0/1.
    for d in range(1, n):
        delta = 0
        ii = i + d
        if ii < n:
            delta -= 1 - h[ii]
        ii = i - d
        if ii >= 0:
            delta += h[ii]
        jj = j + d
        if jj < n:
            delta += 1 - h[jj]
        jj = j - d
        if jj >= 0:
            delta -= h[jj]
        if delta:
            newc[center + d] += delta
        delta = 0
        ii = i - d
        if ii >= 0:
            delta -= 1 - h[ii]
        ii = i + d
        if ii < n:
            delta += h[ii]
        jj = j - d
        if jj >= 0:
            delta += 1 - h[jj]
        jj = j + d
        if jj < n:
            delta -= h[jj]
        if delta:
            newc[center - d] += delta
    dist = i - j
    if dist < 0: dist = -dist
    newc[center + dist] += 1
    newc[center - dist] += 1
    return newc, int(newc.max())
```
Can optimize by precomputing `h_i_neighbors`? Fine.

But note `1 - h[ii]` with h int gives int; if h dtype int8, 1-h int8. We'll use int64.

`binary_full_scan_once(c,h)`:
```
def binary_full_scan_once(c,h):
    n=h.size; center=n-1
    ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
    cur = int(c.max())
    best = cur; bi=-1; bj=-1; bestc=None
    for i in ones:
        # if cur already 0 break
        for j in zeros:
            newc, s = binary_candidate(c,h,i,j,n)
            if s < best:
                best = s; bi=i; bj=j; bestc=newc
                if best <= 1: # maybe continue? We can break for speed if target? Not necessary.
                    pass
    if bi >= 0:
        h[bi]=0; h[bj]=1
        c = bestc
        return True, c, best
    return False, c, cur
```
This recomputes `c.max()` once; but if bestc from candidate based on old c, after applying, max=best. Good.

Potential issue: In full scan, `binary_candidate` uses h old. For each candidate, h unchanged. Good.

But if `bestc` is a view? It is copy. Good.

`binary_random_swap(c,h,rng,n)`:
```
def binary_random_swap(c,h,rng,n):
    while True:
       x=int(rng.integers(0,n)); y=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if x != y and h[x] != h[y]: break
    if h[x] == 1:
       i=x; j=y
    else:
       i=y; j=x
    newc,s=binary_candidate(c,h,i,j,n)
    h[i]=0; h[j]=1; c=newc
    return s
```
We need ensure no infinite if no zeros/ones; balanced even so okay.

`make_binary_balanced(h,n)`:
```
def make_binary_balanced(h,n):
    arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    b = (arr >= 0.5).astype(np.int64)
    target = n//2
    s = int(b.sum())
    if s < target:
       idxs = np.where(b == 0)[0]
       # choose largest arr
       order = np.argsort(-arr[idxs])
       for k in range(target-s): b[idxs[order[k]]]=1
    elif s > target:
       idxs = np.where(b == 1)[0]
       order = np.argsort(arr[idxs])
       for k in range(s-target): b[idxs[order[k]]]=0
    return b
```
If n odd and target half not integer, for binary not.

`random_binary(n,rng)`:
```
arr=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int64); arr[:n//2]=1; rng.shuffle(arr); return arr
```
`modular_binary(n,a,b)`:
```
idx=np.arange(n); p=(a*idx+b)%n; return (p < n//2).astype(np.int64)
```

Continuous `pair_cd` code details:
```
def pair_cd(h, time_limit, rng):
    n = h.size
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = project_box_sum(h, target).copy()
    M = _score_float(h)
    best_h = h.copy(); best_M = M
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    grid=7
    pairs_cycle = max(50, min(300, 6*n))
    stall=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
       improved=False
       for _ in range(pairs_cycle):
          if time.time() > deadline: break
          i = int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
          if i == j: continue
          s = h[i] + h[j]
          lo = max(0.0, s-1.0); hi = min(1.0, s)
          if hi - lo < 1e-8: continue
          oi = h[i]; oj = s - oi
          # If current pair sum s but h[j] may not equal s-oi? it does.
          best_x = oi; best_m = M; best_y = oj
          # build xs: linspace plus current
          xs = np.linspace(lo, hi, grid)
          # add current if outside? current within.
          for x in xs:
             x = float(x)
             y = s - x
             if x < 0 or x > 1 or y < 0 or y > 1: continue
             if abs(x - oi) < 1e-12: continue
             h[i] = x; h[j] = y
             m = _score_float(h)
             if m < best_m - 1e-12:
                best_m = m; best_x = x; best_y = y
          h[i]=best_x; h[j]=best_y
          if best_m < M - 1e-12:
             M = best_m; improved = True
             if M < best_M:
                best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
       if improved:
          stall=0
       else:
          stall += 1
          if stall > 4:
             # perturb
             scale = 0.05
             noise = rng.normal(scale=scale, size=n)
             noise -= noise.mean()
             y = project_box_sum(h + noise, target)
             yM = _score_float(y)
             if yM < best_M + 0.15:
                h = y; M = yM
             else:
                h = best_h.copy(); M = best_M
             stall=0
       # occasional exact sum
       if stall % 10 == 0:
           h = enforce_exact_sum(h, target)
    h = enforce_exact_sum(h, target)
    # If current best_M from h may differ due final sum
    if M >= best_M:
       h = best_h
    h = enforce_exact_sum(h, target)
    return h, (2.0/n) * _score_float(h)
```
Need be careful: In candidate evaluation, if we set h to candidate and evaluate, after loop set best. But if best not improved, we set h to orig (best_x=oi). But if `oi` not exactly within [lo,hi] due float, okay. If `best_x` from xs and y=s-x might slightly outside due rounding; clip? We check.

Let's refine `binary_optimize`:
```
def binary_optimize(h, time_limit, rng):
   n = h.size
   h = make_binary_balanced(h,n)
   c = initial_binary_c(h)
   cur = int(c.max())
   best = cur; best_h = h.copy()
   start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
   rounds=0
   while time.time() < deadline:
      # hill climb
      progress = True
      while progress and time.time() < deadline:
         progress, c, cur = binary_full_scan_once(c,h)
         if progress:
            if cur < best:
               best=cur; best_h=h.copy()
         else:
            break
      # if no time break
      if time.time() >= deadline: break
      # perturb current best or current? Use current local optimum.
      iterations = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
      # If many stalls, use more
      h = best_h.copy(); c = initial_binary_c(h); cur = best
      for _ in range(iterations):
          if time.time() >= deadline: break
          cur = binary_random_swap(c,h,rng,n)
      # If perturbation too badly worse, maybe discard
      if cur > best + iterations + 2:
          h = best_h.copy(); c=initial_binary_c(h); cur=best
      rounds +=1
   return best_h, float(best)
```

def scipy_continuous(h, time_limit, rng):
   n=h.size; target=n/2.0
   h=project_box_sum(h,target).copy()
   global _G_N,_G_BETA,_G_PEN,_G_TARGET
   _G_N=n; _G_TARGET=target; _G_BETA=2.0; _G_PEN=5.0
   start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
   # Use maxiter estimated: 1000
   try:
      res = scopt.minimize(function=objective_penalty, x0=h, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B',
             bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n, options={'maxiter':2000, 'maxls':20, 'ftol':1e-10, 'gtol':1e-8, 'disp':False})
   except Exception: res=None
   if res is not None and res.x is not None:
       h2 = project_box_sum(res.x, target)
       c2 = (2/n)*score_count(h2)
       return h2,c2
   return h,(2/n)*score_count(h)
```

def enforce_exact_sum(h,target):
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   for _ in range(5):
     d=target-h.sum()
     if abs(d)<1e-12: break
     if d>0:

       if idxs.size==0: break
       # distribute d over idxs with capacity
       cap=1-h[idxs]
       # order by largest cap
       order=np.argsort(-cap)
       for idx in idxs[order]: ...
     else: similar
   # final adjust a variable with slack

Let's write `smooth_grad` carefully:
```
def smooth_grad(h, beta):
    n = h.size
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    M = float(c.max())
    z = beta*(c - M)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = e.sum()
    if s == 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros_like(c); w[n-1]=1.0
    else:
        w = e / s
    grad = (1.0 - 2.0*h) * w[n-1]
    for r in range(1,n):
        wp = w[n-1+r]; wn = w[n-1-r]
        if wp + wn <= 1e-15: continue
        grad[:r] += wn
        grad[r:] += wp
        grad[:r] -= (wp + wn) * h[r:]
        grad[r:] -= (wp + wn) * h[:-r]
    return M, grad
```
Check linear for c array? c_full index +r = np lag +r = h[q+r](1-h[q]). Derivative wrt h[j]: if j as h-position: j = q+r -> q=j-r valid if j>=r: weight wp. if j as one_minus: q=j valid if j+r<n (j<r): weight -? Wait derivative of c_{+r}: sum h[q+r](1-h[q]). Derivative wrt h[j]:
- if j is h-position (j = q+r): q=j-r, contribution + (1 - h[q]).
- if j is one_minus (q=j): contribution - h[j+r].
My earlier formula used `linear +wp for j>=r` and product term -wp*(h[j-r] if j>=r + h[j+r] if j<r)?? That was for different orientation (h[t](1-h[t+d])). Need correct gradient for np ordering! Important. We must fix smooth gradient.

Let's derive proper gradient for c_{+r}=Σ_{q=0}^{n-1-r} h[q+r]*(1-h[q]).
Derivative wrt h[j]:
Term types:
1. h-position j = q+r => q=j-r ∈ [0,n-1-r] -> j∈[r,n-1]. Contribution +(1 - h[j-r]).
2. one_minus-position q=j -> q∈[0,n-1-r] -> j∈[0,n-1-r] (j<r). Contribution - h[j+r].
Thus derivative = wp*[ (1-h[j-r]) for j≥r ] - wp*[ h[j+r] for j<r ].
Similarly c_{-r}=Σ h[q-r]*(1-h[q]) where q from r..n-1, p=q-r.
Derivative:
- h-position p=j -> j = q-r, q=j+r, valid j∈[0,n-1-r] (j<r): contribution +(1 - h[j+r]).
- one_minus q=j -> q∈[r,n-1] (j≥r): contribution - h[j-r].
So derivative for wn: +(1-h[j+r]) if j<r; -h[j-r] if j≥r.
This is different. We can express:
For +r:
   add wp*(1 - neighbor) to j>=r; subtract wp*neighbor to j<r.
For -r:
   add wn*(1 - neighbor) to j<r; subtract wn*neighbor to j>=r.
Where neighbor for h-position/one? Let's combine.
For each r:
 product auto? We can write contribution as (wp for j>=r plus wn for j<r)*(1 - neighbor) - (wp for j<r plus wn for j>=r)*(neighbor). Here neighbor is the other position at distance r.
For j<r:
  other = j+r.
  + terms: wp? j>=r false; wn? j<r true => +wn*(1-h[j+r])
  - terms: wp? j<r true => -wp*h[j+r]; wn? j>=r false.
  total = (wn - wp)*h? Wait +wn(1-h) - wp h = wn - (wn+wp)h.
For j>=r:
  other = j-r.
  + terms: wp true +wp(1-h); wn false.
  - terms: wn true -wn*h.
  total = wp - (wp+wn)h.
Thus gradient update:
```
wsum = wp+wn
# initialize add vector? For j<r: grad += wn - wsum*h[r:]
For j>=r: grad += wp - wsum*h[:-r]
```
Can implement vectorized:
```
grad[:r] += wn - wsum*h[r:]
grad[r:] += wp - wsum*h[:-r]
```
This naturally includes product. For r=1..n-1. Nice.
For c0: c0=Σ h(1-h), grad 1-2h.
Let's test with simple h=[1,0], n=2. c = [1,0,0], M=1, β high weights center-1 only (wn for r=1 =1, wp=0). Grad r=1:
 grad[:1] += wn -0*h[1:] =1
 grad[1:] +=0 -0=0
 c0 weight0. Grad [1,0]. Objective max includes lag -1; to reduce it? h=[1,0], max1. If decrease h0, objective decreases? h0=0.5,h1=0 sum not fixed; c-1=0.5, c+1=0? max0.5, gradient +1 (increase h0) is wrong for minimization? Wait gradient of c_{-1}=h0(1-h1) wrt h0 =1; at h0=1, increasing h0 impossible (bound), L-BFGS-B bound prevents. For min with bounds, gradient positive at upper bound, okay. If sum penalty may move. Fine.
For h=[0.5,0.5], c both? c_-1: h0(1-h1)=0.25, c_+1: h1(1-h0)=0.25, c0=0.5? β low weights all. Gradient formula okay.

Detailed code:
```
def exact_sum(h, target):
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    if h.size == 0: return h
    for _ in range(6):
        d = float(target) - float(h.sum())
        if abs(d) < 1e-13: break
        if d > 0:
            idx = np.where(h < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
            if idx.size == 0: break
            cap = (1.0 - h[idx])
            order = np.argsort(-cap)
            for ii in idx[order]:
                if d <= 1e-13: break
                add = min(float(d), float(1.0 - h[ii]))
                h[ii] += add
                d -= add
        else:
            idx = np.where(h > 1e-12)[0]
            if idx.size == 0: break
            cap = h[idx]
            order = np.argsort(cap)
            for ii in idx[order]:
                if d >= -1e-13: break
                sub = min(float(-d), float(h[ii]))
                h[ii] -= sub
                d += sub
    # final nudge one coordinate if very small residual
    d = float(target) - float(h.sum())
    if abs(d) > 1e-14:
        # choose coordinate with most slack in sign direction
        if d > 0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
            add = min(1.0 - h[idx], d)
            h[idx] += add
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            sub = min(h[idx], -d)
            h[idx] -= sub
    return np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
```
This may not make np.sum exact due add min; but residual tiny.

`project_box_sum`:
```
def project_box_sum(x, target):
    x = np.clip(x,0,1)
    target=float(target)
    if x.size == 0: return x
    if abs(x.sum()-target) < 1e-13:
       return exact_sum(x,target)
    lo = -1.0; hi = 1.0
    for _ in range(42):
        tau=0.5*(lo+hi)
        y=np.clip(x-tau,0.0,1.0)
        if y.sum() < target:
           hi=tau
        else:
           lo=tau
    y=np.clip(x-0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    return exact_sum(y,target)
```
If target outside [0,n]? not.

`score_float` and `score_int`:

def score_float(h):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')))
def score_int(h):
    return int(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode='full')))
```

binary make:
```
def make_binary_balanced(h, n):
    arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    b = (arr >= 0.5).astype(np.int64)
    target = n // 2
    s = int(b.sum())
    if s < target:
        idx = np.nonzero(b == 0)[0]
        if idx.size:
           order = np.argsort(-arr[idx])
           for k in range(target - s): b[idx[order[k]]] = 1
    elif s > target:
        idx = np.nonzero(b == 1)[0]
        order = np.argsort(arr[idx])
        for k in range(s - target): b[idx[order[k]]] = 0
    return b
```
If initial h continuous, binary rounding may not preserve sum? It enforces count.

`random_binary`:
```
def random_binary(n,rng):
   a=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int64); a[:n//2]=1; rng.shuffle(a); return a
```
`modular_binary`:
```
def modular_binary(n,a,b):
   idx=np.arange(n dtype int64); p=(a*idx+b)%n; bnp=(p < n//2).astype(np.int64); return make_binary_balanced(bnp,n)
```
`select_binary_start`:
```
def select_binary_start(n, rng, count=60):
   best_h=None; best_s=None
   idx=np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
   for k in range(count):
      if k % 5 == 0:
         h=random_binary(n,rng)
      else:
         a=int(abs(rng.integers(1, max(2, n))) | 1)
         b=int(rng.integers(0, n))
         h=make_binary_balanced(((a*idx+b)%n < n//2).astype(np.int64), n)
      s=score_int(h)
      if best_s is None or s < best_s:
         best_s=s; best_h=h.copy()
   return best_h
```
Need `rng.integers(1, max(2,n))` if n>1. n≥48.

`binary_candidate` with corrected formulas. Use local `h_arr=h`, `c_arr=c`. In loop, to reduce attribute lookup, assign `hd=h` etc.
```
def binary_candidate(c, h, i, j, n):
    center = n - 1
    newc = c.copy()
    for d in range(1, n):
        delta = 0
        q = i - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta -= 1 - h[q]
        q = i + d
        if q < n:
            delta += h[q]
        q = j - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta += 1 - h[q]
        q = j + d
        if q < n:
            delta -= h[q]
        if delta:
            newc[center + d] += delta
        delta = 0
        q = i + d
        if q < n:
            delta -= 1 - h[q]
        q = i - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta += h[q]
        q = j + d
        if q < n:
            delta += 1 - h[q]
        q = j - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta -= h[q]
        if delta:
            newc[center - d] += delta
    dist = i - j
    if dist < 0: dist = -dist
    newc[center + dist] += 1
    newc[center - dist] += 1
    return newc, int(newc.max())
```
Check negative formulas with corrected c: For -d: i as h-position if q=i+d valid (dest q? For c_{-d}=h[q-d](1-h[q]), h-position p=q-d. If i=p, q=i+d valid => δ_i*(1-h[i+d]) = -(1-h), correct. one_minus q=i, p=i-d valid => -δ_i*h[i-d] = +h, correct. j δ=+1: h-position + (1-h[j+d]); one_minus -h[j-d]. This matches code? Code: q=i+d delta -=1-h; q=i-d delta +=h; q=j+d delta +=1-h; q=j-d delta -=h. Yes.
For +d code: q=i-d delta -=1-h (h-position, correct); q=i+d delta +=h (one_minus, correct); q=j-d delta +=1-h; q=j-d? wait j one_minus q=j+d delta -=h. Code yes. Good.

But for +d, h-position for i: q=i-d (destination one_minus), yes. Good.

`binary_full_scan_once`:
```
def binary_full_scan_once(c, h):
    n = h.size
    ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
    cur = int(c.max())
    best = cur; bi = -1; bj = -1; bestc = None
    for i in ones:
       for j in zeros:
          newc, s = binary_candidate(c, h, i, j, n)
          if s < best:
             best = s; bi = i; bj = j; bestc = newc
             if best <= 0: return True, newc, 0
    if bi >= 0:
       h[bi] = 0; h[bj] = 1
       return True, bestc, best
    return False, c, cur
```
`best <= 0` impossible unless n=1. Fine.

`binary_random_swap`:
```
def binary_random_swap(c,h,rng,n):
    while True:
       x=int(rng.integers(0,n)); y=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if x != y and h[x] != h[y]: break
    if h[x] == 1:
       i=x; j=y
    else:
       i=y; j=x
    newc,s=binary_candidate(c,h,i,j,n)
    h[i]=0; h[j]=1; c=newc
    return s
```
`binary_optimize`:
```
def binary_optimize(h, time_limit, rng):
    n = h.size
    h = make_binary_balanced(h, n).copy()
    c = binary_initial_c(h)
    cur = int(c.max())
    best = cur; best_h = h.copy()
    start = time.time(); deadline = start + time_limit
    moves = 0
    rounds = 0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        progress = True
        while progress and time.time() < deadline:
            progress, c, cur = binary_full_scan_once(c, h)
            if progress:
                moves += 1
                if cur < best:
                    best = cur; best_h = h.copy()
                if moves % 32 == 0:
                    c = binary_initial_c(h)
                    cur = int(c.max())
        if time.time() >= deadline: break
        # perturb
        h = best_h.copy(); c = binary_initial_c(h); cur = int(c.max())
        k = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
        if best - cur? cur==best
        for _ in range(k):
            if time.time() >= deadline: break
            cur = binary_random_swap(c, h, rng, n)
        # if perturbation did not create plausible path, revert
        if cur > best + k + 2:
            h = best_h.copy(); c = binary_initial_c(h); cur = best
        rounds += 1
    if time.time() < deadline:
       # final hill
       progress=True
       while progress and time.time() < deadline:
          progress,c,cur=binary_full_scan_once(c,h)
          if progress and cur<best: best=cur;best_h=h.copy()
    return best_h, float(best)
```
This repeats full scan until no improvement. If no improvement from best, perturb. Good.

Potential issue: If after no improvement from best, we perturb best, then next hill might improve the perturbed state but not surpass best; we still spend time. Good.

Smooth_grad and objective:
```
def smooth_grad(h, beta): ...
_G_N=0; ...
def objective_penalty(x):
   n=_G_N
   M, grad = smooth_grad(x, _G_BETA)
   if _G_PEN != 0:
      s = x.sum() - _G_TARGET
      M += _G_PEN * s * s
      grad = grad + (2.0 * _G_PEN * s)
   return M, grad
```
Note if `_G_PEN` is int, grad shape. If x not finite? L-BFGS-B x finite.

`scipy_continuous`:
```
def scipy_continuous(h, time_limit, rng):
    n=h.size; target=0.5*n
    h0=project_box_sum(h,target)
    global _G_N,_G_BETA,_G_PEN,_G_TARGET
    _G_N=n; _G_TARGET=target
    best_h=h0.copy(); best_c=(2.0/n)*score_float(h0)
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    for beta,pen,iters in ((2.0,1.0,400),(2.0,10.0,400),(1.0,1.0,400)):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       _G_BETA=beta; _G_PEN=pen
       try:
          res=scopt.minimize(objective_penalty, h0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B',
                             bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n,
                             options={'maxiter':iters, 'maxls':30, 'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-9,'disp':False})
       except Exception:
          res=None
       if res is not None and hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None:
          h2=project_box_sum(res.x,target)
          c2=(2.0/n)*score_float(h2)
          if c2<best_c:
             best_c=c2; best_h=h2.copy()
             h0=h2
       if time.time()>deadline: break
    return best_h,best_c
```
This may use time; maxiter 400*3. For n=47, okay. If penalty large, `smooth_grad` with beta=2 and penalty. Good.

Pair_cd can use `score_float`. The pair_list length 1128; constructing list of tuples okay. It may use memory but small.

`pair_cd` with full sweep and candidate list:
```
def pair_cd(h, time_limit, rng):
    n=h.size; target=0.5*n
    h=project_box_sum(h,target).copy()
    M=score_float(h); best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+time_limit
    pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i+1,n)]
    stall=0
    while time.time()<deadline:
       improved=False
       for (i,j) in pairs:
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          s = h[i]+h[j]
          lo=max(0.0,s-1.0); hi=min(1.0,s)
          if hi-lo<1e-10: continue
          oi=h[i]
          best_x=oi; best_m=M; best_y=s-oi
          # candidates: endpoints/fractions and small eps
          cands = [lo, hi]
          for f in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9): cands.append(lo + f*(hi-lo))
          for e in (1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,1e-1,3e-1,0.5):
              cands.append(oi+e); cands.append(oi-e)
          for x in cands:
              x=float(x)
              if x < lo - 1e-12 or x > hi + 1e-12: continue
              x = min(max(x, lo), hi)
              if abs(x - oi) < 1e-12: continue
              y=s-x
              if y < -1e-12 or y > 1+1e-12: continue
              y = min(max(y,0.0),1.0)
              h[i]=x; h[j]=y
              m=score_float(h)
              if m < best_m - 1e-12:
                 best_m=m; best_x=x; best_y=s-x
          h[i]=best_x; h[j]=best_y if improved? Need if best_x changed: h[j]=s-best_x else oj. Since best_y initialized s-oi; set h[j]=best_y.
          if best_m < M - 1e-12:
             M=best_m; improved=True
             if M < best_M:
                best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
       if improved:
          stall=0
       else:
          stall += 1
          if stall >= 3:
             scale=0.05
             noise=rng.normal(scale=scale,size=n); noise -= noise.mean()
             y=project_box_sum(h+noise,target)
             yM=score_float(y)
             if yM < best_M + 0.1:
                h=y; M=yM
             else:
                h=best_h.copy(); M=best_M
             stall=0
    if M >= best_M:
       h=best_h
    h=project_box_sum(h, target)
    return h, (2.0/n)*score_float(h)
```
This will run full pair sweep each while. If time 120s and n=47, maybe many sweeps? Full sweep ~1128 pairs*~15 candidates=16920 score calls. Each score call correlate 47; 17k* maybe 0.1ms=1.7s. 120s ~70 sweeps. Great.

But candidate list with eps includes many invalid/outside; check. For s=1, oi maybe 0 or1, lo=0,hi=1. cands many duplicates; okay.

Potential performance: Creating `cands` list for each pair allocation heavy. We can precompute fraction list, eps list top-level? Define module constants `FRAC=(0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9)`, `EPS=...`. No closures. That's fine. Or inside function. Keep small.

Could reduce candidates to 10 for speed. Use 7: 0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9 and eps 0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2 around current. Need small 1e-3. Let's define `PAIR_FRACS = (0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9)` and `PAIR_EPS = (0.001, 0.005, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4)`. Candidates up to 2+5+14=21. Good.

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    start = time.time()
    deadline = start + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_h = None; best_c = float('inf'); best_n = 0
    def consider? no closures. Inline.
    try: init = initial_h_values
    except NameError: init = None
    if init is not None:
       h = np.asarray(init, dtype=float).copy()
       n = h.size
       if n > 0:
          target=0.5*n
          h=project_box_sum(h,target)
          c=(2.0/n)*score_float(h)
          if c < best_c: best_c=c; best_h=h.copy(); best_n=n
          rem=deadline-time.time()
          if rem > 40:
             h2,c2 = pair_cd(h, min(120.0, rem*0.5), rng)
             if c2 < best_c: update
    # binary
    # Choose n list maybe depending best_c: if best_c already <0.38 maybe still try lower.
    for n in (48, 50, 52, 54, 56, 60, 64):
       rem=deadline-time.time()
       if rem < 80: break
       # Time allocation: invest more if n close to target? Use 120 sec for first two, 80 for rest.
       if n <= 52: lim = min(220.0, rem)
       elif n <= 60: lim = min(120.0, rem)
       else: lim = min(80.0, rem)
       if lim < 30: break
       h0 = select_binary_start(n, rng, 40)
       bh, bm = binary_optimize(h0, lim, rng)
       c = (2.0 / n) * bm
       if c < best_c - 1e-12:
          best_c = c; best_h = bh.astype(float).copy(); best_n = n
       rem=deadline-time.time()
       # optional continuous polish on this binary: if rem > 60 and n<=64
       if rem > 60:
          h3,c3 = pair_cd(bh.astype(float).copy(), min(60.0, rem*0.5), rng)
          if c3 < best_c: update
    # scipy continuous on best if rem >80
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem > 60 and best_h is not None and best_n <= 100:
       h4,c4 = scipy_continuous(best_h.astype(float).copy(), min(100.0, rem*0.6), rng)
       if c4 < best_c: update
    # fallback if no best
    if best_h is None:
       n=48
       h=select_binary_start(n, rng, 20)
       bh,bm=binary_optimize(h, max(1.0, deadline-time.time()), rng)
       best_h=bh.astype(float); best_c=(2.0/n)*bm; best_n=n
    # final ensure constraints
    if best_h is not None:
       n=best_n
       target=0.5*n
       best_h=project_box_sum(best_h,target)
       best_c=(2.0/n)*score_float(best_h)
       # if final c worse than previous due rounding? We still return; but could be >. If previous binary int, project no change. If continuous, project may change slightly. Use score after project.
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```

def resample_for_n(h, n):
    if h.size == 0: return None
    if h.size == n: return h.astype(float)
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,h.size)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    return np.interp(xn,xo,h)

Could compute `score_tie_float(h)` returning (max, active sum) by `c=np.correlate...; M=c.max(); t=sum(c[c>=M-1e-6])`. This doubles code allocation. Not.

Given target small, pair_cd may just need max decrease; if current record 0.38085, to get 0.3808 max count decreases by 0.0001, strict works. If no max decrease, plateau moves not needed.

Now, if `initial_h_values` has record exactly 0.38085 maybe max count 8.959. Pair_cd grid step 0.001 changes M by maybe ~0.001, enough.

Let's finalize code. Need ensure no stray indentation. Use only one fence.

Potential final c after project: If best_h binary int converted float and project exact, score same. If continuous and project changes score slightly above best_c, we recompute and return maybe worse than saved best_c? In run we keep best_c from before project. At final, we project and recompute; maybe c slightly up but still below target. But if recompute >best_c due rounding, should we return recompute? The returned h must correspond. We could keep best_h from before final project if final recompute worse? But need exact constraints. We can try `h_check,c_check=evaluate_candidate(best_h,best_n)`. If c_check > best_c + 1e-9, maybe try snapped version? Simpler return h_check,c_check. The difference tiny. If it causes target fail, hmm. We can avoid final project if sum already exact: `if abs(best_h.sum()-best_n/2) < 1e-10 and (min>=0 max<=1): use as is; else project`. Evaluate candidate already projects. Our `best_h` from pair_cd/project sum exact within; binary exact. So final evaluate no change. We'll do:

   if best_h.size and (np.min(best_h)<-1e-12 or np.max(best_h)>1+1e-12 or abs(best_h.sum()-0.5*best_n)>1e-9):

If binary int converted float, sum exact. Good.

No `evaluate_candidate` min/max? We'll just final.

Let's now provide code. Need ensure imports include `from scipy import optimize as scopt`; if scipy not, fail but allowed.

```python
import time
import math
import numpy as np
from scipy import optimize as scopt

_G_N = 0
_G_BETA = 2.0
_G_PEN = 0.0
_G_TARGET = 0.0
_PAIR_FRACS = (0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9)
_PAIR_EPS = (0.001, 0.005, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4)


def score_float(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")))


def score_int(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.int64)
    return int(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode="full")))


def enforce_exact_sum(h, target):
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    target = float(target)
    if h.size == 0:
        return h
    for _ in range(6):
        d = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(d) < 1e-13:
            break
        if d > 0:
            idx = np.where(h < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
            if idx.size == 0:
                break
            order = np.argsort(-(1.0 - h[idx]))
            for ii in idx[order]:
                if d <= 1e-13:
                    break
                add = min(d, 1.0 - float(h[ii]))
                h[ii] += add
                d -= add
        else:
            idx = np.where(h > 1e-12)[0]
            if idx.size == 0:
                break
            order = np.argsort(h[idx])
            for ii in idx[order]:
                if d >= -1e-13:
                    break
                sub = min(-d, float(h[ii]))
                h[ii] -= sub
                d += sub
    d = target - float(h.sum())
    if abs(d) > 1e-14:
        if d > 0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
            h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + d)
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] + d)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def project_box_sum(x, target):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(x, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    target = float(target)
    if x.size == 0:
        return x
    if abs(float(x.sum()) - target) < 1e-13:
        return enforce_exact_sum(x, target)
    lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
    for _ in range(42):
        tau = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        y = np.clip(x - tau, 0.0, 1.0)
        if float(y.sum()) < target:
            hi = tau
        else:
            lo = tau
    y = np.clip(x - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    return enforce_exact_sum(y, target)


def make_binary_balanced(h, n):
    arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    b = (arr >= 0.5).astype(np.int64)
    target = n // 2
    s = int(b.sum())
    if s < target:
        idx = np.nonzero(b == 0)[0]
        if idx.size:
            order = np.argsort(-arr[idx])
            for k in range(target - s):
                b[idx[order[k]]] = 1
    elif s > target:
        idx = np.nonzero(b == 1)[0]
        if idx.size:
            order = np.argsort(arr[idx])
            for k in range(s - target):
                b[idx[order[k]]] = 0
    return b


def random_binary(n, rng):
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    a[: n // 2] = 1
    rng.shuffle(a)
    return a


def resample_for_n(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if h.size == n:
        return h.copy()
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, h.size)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.interp(xn, xo, h)


def select_binary_start(n, rng, count=50):
    best_h = None
    best_s = None
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    for k in range(count):
        if k % 5 == 0:
            h = random_binary(n, rng)
        else:
            a = int(abs(rng.integers(1, max(2, n))) | 1)
            b = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            h = make_binary_balanced(((a * idx + b) % n < (n // 2)).astype(np.int64), n)
        s = score_int(h)
        if best_s is None or s < best_s:
            best_s = s
            best_h = h.copy()
    return best_h


def binary_initial_c(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode="full").astype(np.int64)


def binary_candidate(c, h, i, j, n):
    center = n - 1
    newc = c.copy()
    for d in range(1, n):
        delta = 0

        q = i - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta -= 1 - h[q]
        q = i + d
        if q < n:
            delta += h[q]
        q = j - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta += 1 - h[q]
        q = j + d
        if q < n:
            delta -= h[q]
        if delta:
            newc[center + d] += delta

        delta = 0
        q = i + d
        if q < n:
            delta -= 1 - h[q]
        q = i - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta += h[q]
        q = j + d
        if q < n:
            delta += 1 - h[q]
        q = j - d
        if q >= 0:
            delta -= h[q]
        if delta:
            newc[center - d] += delta

    dist = i - j
    if dist < 0:
        dist = -dist
    newc[center + dist] += 1
    newc[center - dist] += 1
    return newc, int(np.max(newc))


def binary_full_scan_once(c, h):
    n = h.size
    ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
    zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
    cur = int(np.max(c))
    best = cur
    best_cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(c == cur))
    bi = -1
    bj = -1
    bestc = None

    for i in ones:
        for j in zeros:
            newc, s = binary_candidate(c, h, i, j, n)
            cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(newc == s))
            if s < best or (s == best and cnt < best_cnt):
                best = s
                best_cnt = cnt
                bi = i
                bj = j
                bestc = newc

    if bi >= 0:
        h[bi] = 0
        h[bj] = 1
        return True, bestc, best
    return False, c, cur


def binary_random_swap(c, h, rng, n):
    while True:
        x = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        y = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        if x != y and h[x] != h[y]:
            break
    if h[x] == 1:
        i, j = x, y
    else:
        i, j = y, x
    newc, s = binary_candidate(c, h, i, j, n)
    h[i] = 0
    h[j] = 1
    return newc, s


def binary_optimize(h, time_limit, rng):
    n = h.size
    h = make_binary_balanced(h, n).copy()
    c = binary_initial_c(h)
    cur = int(np.max(c))
    best = cur
    best_h = h.copy()

    start = time.time()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(time_limit))
    moves = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        progress = True
        while progress and time.time() < deadline:
            progress, c, cur = binary_full_scan_once(c, h)
            if progress:
                moves += 1
                if cur < best:
                    best = cur
                    best_h = h.copy()
                if moves % 32 == 0:
                    c = binary_initial_c(h)
                    cur = int(np.max(c))

        if time.time() >= deadline:
            break

        h = best_h.copy()
        c = binary_initial_c(h)
        cur = int(np.max(c))

        k = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
        for _ in range(k):
            if time.time() >= deadline:
                break
            c, cur = binary_random_swap(c, h, rng, n)

        if cur > best + k + 2:
            h = best_h.copy()
            c = binary_initial_c(h)
            cur = int(np.max(c))

    progress = True
    while progress and time.time() < deadline:
        progress, c, cur = binary_full_scan_once(c, h)
        if progress and cur < best:
            best = cur
            best_h = h.copy()

    return best_h, float(best)


def smooth_grad(h, beta):
    n = h.size
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = beta * (c - m)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[n - 1] = 1.0
    else:
        w = e / s

    grad = (1.0 - 2.0 * h) * w[n - 1]
    for r in range(1, n):
        wp = w[n - 1 + r]
        wn = w[n - 1 - r]
        ws = wp + wn
        if ws <= 1e-15:
            continue
        grad[:r] += wn - ws * h[r:]
        grad[r:] += wp - ws * h[:-r]
    return m, grad


def objective_penalty(x):
    n = _G_N
    m, grad = smooth_grad(np.asarray(x, dtype=float), _G_BETA)
    if _G_PEN != 0.0:
        s = float(np.sum(x)) - _G_TARGET
        m += _G_PEN * s * s
        grad = grad + (2.0 * _G_PEN * s)
    return m, grad


def scipy_continuous(h, time_limit, rng):
    n = h.size
    target = 0.5 * n
    h0 = project_box_sum(h, target)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_c = (2.0 / n) * score_float(h0)

    global _G_N, _G_BETA, _G_PEN, _G_TARGET
    _G_N = n
    _G_TARGET = target

    start = time.time()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(time_limit))

    for beta, pen, iters in ((2.0, 1.0, 350), (2.0, 8.0, 350), (1.0, 1.0, 350)):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        _G_BETA = beta
        _G_PEN = pen
        try:
            res = scopt.minimize(
                objective_penalty,
                h0,
                jac=True,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                bounds=[(0.0, 1.0)] * n,
                options={"maxiter": iters, "maxls": 25, "ftol": 1e-12,
                         "gtol": 1e-9, "disp": False},
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None
        if res is not None and hasattr(res, "x") and res.x is not None:
            h2 = project_box_sum(res.x, target)
            c2 = (2.0 / n) * score_float(h2)
            if c2 < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = c2
                best_h = h2.copy()
                h0 = h2
    return best_h, best_c


def pair_cd(h, time_limit, rng):
    n = h.size
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = project_box_sum(h, target).copy()
    m = score_float(h)
    best_m = m
    best_h = h.copy()

    start = time.time()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(time_limit))
    pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i + 1, n)]
    stall = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        improved = False
        for i, j in pairs:
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            s = float(h[i]) + float(h[j])
            lo = max(0.0, s - 1.0)
            hi = min(1.0, s)
            if hi - lo < 1e-10:
                continue

            oi = float(h[i])
            best_x = oi
            best_y = s - oi
            best_m_trial = m

            cands = [lo, hi]
            span = hi - lo
            for f in _PAIR_FRACS:
                cands.append(lo + f * span)
            for e in _PAIR_EPS:
                cands.append(oi + e)
                cands.append(oi - e)

            for x in cands:
                x = float(x)
                if x < lo - 1e-12 or x > hi + 1e-12:
                    continue
                x = min(max(x, lo), hi)
                if abs(x - oi) < 1e-12:
                    continue
                y = s - x
                if y < -1e-12 or y > 1.0 + 1e-12:
                    continue
                y = min(max(y, 0.0), 1.0)
                h[i] = x
                h[j] = y
                val = score_float(h)
                if val < best_m_trial - 1e-12:
                    best_m_trial = val
                    best_x = x
                    best_y = y

            h[i] = best_x
            h[j] = best_y

            if best_m_trial < m - 1e-12:
                m = best_m_trial
                improved = True
                if m < best_m:
                    best_m = m
                    best_h = h.copy()

        if improved:
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall >= 3:
                noise = rng.normal(scale=0.05, size=n)
                noise -= float(np.mean(noise))
                y = project_box_sum(h + noise, target)
                ym = score_float(y)
                if ym < best_m + 0.1:
                    h = y
                    m = ym
                else:
                    h = best_h.copy()
                    m = best_m
                stall = 0

    if m >= best_m:
        h = best_h
    h = project_box_sum(h, target)
    return h, (2.0 / n) * score_float(h)


def evaluate_candidate(h, n):
    h = project_box_sum(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.5 * n)
    return h, (2.0 / n) * score_float(h)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    start = time.time()
    deadline = start + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_h = None
    best_c = float("inf")
    best_n = 0

    try:
        init = initial_h_values
    except NameError:
        init = None

    if init is not None:
        h0 = np.asarray(init, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = h0.size
        if n0 > 0:
            h0, c0 = evaluate_candidate(h0, n0)
            if c0 < best_c:
                best_c = c0
                best_h = h0.copy()
                best_n = n0

            rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())
            if rem > 45:
                h1, c1 = pair_cd(h0, min(130.0, 0.45 * rem), rng)
                if c1 < best_c:
                    best_c = c1
                    best_h = h1.copy()
                    best_n = n0

    for n in (48, 50, 52, 54, 56, 60, 64):
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())
        if rem < 90:
            break

        if n <= 52:
            lim = min(240.0, rem)
        elif n <= 60:
            lim = min(140.0, rem)
        else:
            lim = min(90.0, rem)

        if lim < 35:
            break

        h0 = select_binary_start(n, rng, 45)
        if best_h is not None:
            hr = resample_for_n(best_h, n)
            hr_bin = make_binary_balanced(hr, n)
            if score_int(hr_bin) < score_int(h0):
                h0 = hr_bin

        bh, bm = binary_optimize(h0, lim, rng)
        c = (2.0 / n) * bm
        if c < best_c:
            best_c = c
            best_h = bh.astype(float).copy()
            best_n = n

        rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())
        if rem > 70:
            hb, cb = pair_cd(bh.astype(float), min(70.0, 0.5 * rem), rng)
            if cb < best_c:
                best_c = cb
                best_h = hb.copy()
                best_n = n

    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.time())
    if rem > 70 and best_h is not None and best_n <= 100:
        hs, cs = scipy_continuous(best_h.astype(float), min(110.0, 0.55 * rem), rng)
        if cs < best_c:
            best_c = cs
            best_h = hs.copy()
            best_n = best_n

    if best_h is None:
        n = 48
        h0 = select_binary_start(n, rng, 25)
        bh, bm = binary_optimize(h0, max(1.0, deadline - time.time()), rng)
        best_h = bh.astype(float).copy()
        best_n = n
        best_c = (2.0 / n) * bm

    best_h = np.asarray(best_h, dtype=float)
    if best_h.size:
        target = 0.5 * best_n
        if (
            float(np.min(best_h)) < -1e-12
            or float(np.max(best_h)) > 1.0 + 1e-12
            or abs(float(np.sum(best_h)) - target) > 1e-9
        ):
            best_h = project_box_sum(best_h, target)
        best_c = (2.0 / best_n) * score_float(best_h)

    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```
